1) TL;DR
The Elm-style architecture is clean and mostly well-implemented, and the split-view/layout system is solid for a first iteration. The main correctness issues are around Unicode handling in search/occurrence selection, selection/cursor invariants, incomplete rectangle selection, and single‑cursor‑centric undo/multi‑cursor behavior, plus a few multi-pane/window-resize inconsistencies and avoidable clones.

---

2) Recommended approach (simple path)

I’ll go by your focus areas and point to concrete locations.

### 1. Architecture quality (Elm-style)

Overall: good separation of `Msg` → `update` → `Cmd` → render, with state in `AppModel`/submodels. Main issues are migration leftovers and multi-pane gaps.

**a. Centralize viewport/resize logic in `EditorArea` instead of only the focused editor**

- Problem: `AppModel::resize` only updates the **focused** editor’s viewport:

  - `model/mod.rs` 151–161:
    ```rust
    pub fn resize(&mut self, width: u32, height: u32) {
        self.window_size = (width, height);

        let text_x = text_start_x(self.char_width).round();
        let visible_columns = ((width as f32 - text_x) / self.char_width).floor() as usize;
        let visible_lines = (height as usize) / self.line_height;

        self.editor_mut()
            .resize_viewport(visible_lines, visible_columns);
    }
    ```

  - Same for `set_char_width` (163–172): only `focused_editor()`’s `viewport.visible_columns` is updated.

- Effect:
  - With split views/multiple editors, non-focused panes keep stale `visible_lines`/`visible_columns`, so scrolling/cursor visibility will be wrong when focus changes.
- Fix (M):
  - Move this logic into an `EditorArea::resize_all_viewports(visible_lines, visible_columns)` that iterates all `self.editors.values_mut()` and updates each `EditorState.viewport`.
  - Call that from `AppModel::resize` and `set_char_width`.
  - Also ensure the layout recomputation happens on resize in the `LayoutMsg`/`AppMsg::Resize` handler (not shown here).

**b. Avoid cloning whole documents just to satisfy the borrow checker**

- Problem:

  - `AppModel::ensure_cursor_visible` (214–221):
    ```rust
    let doc = self
        .editor_area
        .focused_document()
        .expect("must have document")
        .clone();
    self.editor_mut().ensure_cursor_visible(&doc);
    ```

  - `ensure_cursor_visible_directional` does the same (234–241).

- Effect:
  - Clones the entire `Rope` and metadata on every cursor reveal, which will be a real cost on large files.
- Fix (S):
  - Restructure to avoid cloning:

    ```rust
    pub fn ensure_cursor_visible(&mut self) {
        let doc_id = self.editor_area.focused_document_id().expect("must have document");
        let document = self.editor_area.documents.get(&doc_id).expect("doc");
        let editor = self.editor_area.editors.get_mut(
            &self.editor_area.focused_editor_id().expect("editor")
        ).expect("editor");
        editor.ensure_cursor_visible(document);
    }
    ```

  - Or move `ensure_cursor_visible*` helpers onto `EditorArea` which owns both borrows, keeping `AppModel` thinner.

**c. Minor: command architecture is clean**

- `commands.rs` is well-structured; `Cmd::Batch` and `needs_redraw` are idiomatic.
- `update` in `update.rs` (19–32) correctly dispatches on sub-messages and calls a single `sync_status_bar` afterwards, which matches Elm’s “pure update + effects” pattern.

Effort: S–M to clean up; mostly mechanical.

---

### 2. Multi-cursor editing correctness & reverse-order processing

You mentioned reverse-order processing, but the actual Document-editing branches (for `InsertChar`, `Delete*`, etc.) aren’t in the excerpt, so I can’t verify the core loop. However, from what is visible:

**a. Multi-cursor creation & management are mostly sound**

- `EditorState::add_cursor_at` (editor.rs 253–269):
  - Avoids duplicates, initializes a matching `Selection`, and calls `sort_cursors` which keeps cursor/selection zipped and sorted.
- `deduplicate_cursors` (287–310) removes duplicates by `(line, column)` pair and keeps selection arrays in sync.

These are good patterns and set you up to do reverse-order processing safely as long as edits are applied in descending offset order.

**b. Select-next-occurrence has a real correctness bug with multiple occurrences**

- Code: `update.rs` ~334–411 (`EditorMsg::SelectNextOccurrence`).
- Bug: You track `OccurrenceState.last_search_offset`, but you don’t use it when searching; you always search from the **primary cursor**:

  - Lines 359–363:

    ```rust
    let cursor = model.editor().cursor();
    let current_offset = model.document().cursor_to_offset(cursor.line, cursor.column);

    if let Some((start_off, end_off)) = model.document().find_next_occurrence(&search_text, current_offset)
    ```

  - When you add a new occurrence, you only append a cursor (386–387) and **do not** move the primary cursor or re‑sort.

- Failure mode:
  - If the word appears N ≥ 2 times at offsets A < B < C…, then:
    - First `Cmd+J`: selects at A, finds B, adds a cursor at B.
    - Second `Cmd+J`: `cursor()` is still at A; search from `current_offset` again finds B.
    - `already_selected` (369–372) sees a cursor at B and aborts with “No more occurrences”, even though C… exist.

- Fix (S–M):
  - Use `OccurrenceState.last_search_offset` if present:

    ```rust
    let current_offset = if let Some(state) = &model.editor().occurrence_state {
        state.last_search_offset
    } else {
        model.document().cursor_to_offset(cursor.line, cursor.column)
    };
    ```

  - Then update `last_search_offset = end_off` (which you already do at 392–398).
  - Consider also making the “primary cursor” follow the last occurrence (or just rely on `last_search_offset` exclusively) to keep behavior intuitive.

**c. Index-tracking for `UnselectOccurrence` is fragile but acceptable for now**

- `EditorMsg::UnselectOccurrence` (413–442) pops an index from `added_cursor_indices` and removes that cursor/selection if `idx < editor.cursors.len()`.
- This works as long as:
  - You don’t sort or deduplicate cursors between `SelectNextOccurrence` calls.
- Right now that seems true (no `sort_cursors`/`deduplicate_cursors` in that code path), so it’s OK, but document that `added_cursor_indices` are only valid until any reorder/dedup passes.

**d. Reverse-order processing edge cases (to verify in the unseen code)**

You should specifically check your multi-cursor DocumentMsg handlers for:

- Overlapping selections (e.g. two cursors whose selections overlap or touch) – edits must be merged or handled carefully so you don’t double-delete text.
- Mixed insert/delete on same line across cursors—must be applied in **descending offset order** per document to avoid offset drift.
- Multi-line selections and rectangular selections when committing: verify that per-cursor edits are ordered by buffer offset, not just by `(line, column)`.

Effort: S for the `SelectNextOccurrence` bug; M to systematically audit reverse-order logic in DocumentMsg handlers.

---

### 3. Selection system completeness / correctness

The data structures are good (anchor/head, `start()`/`end()`, `is_reversed()`), but for a production-ready selection system there are several gaps.

**a. Unicode safety issues in selection-based search**

- `Selection::get_text` (editor.rs 115–124) itself is fine; it uses `Document::cursor_to_offset` and `Rope::slice` (char-based).
- But the *search* methods that use that text are not Unicode-safe (see section 4a below). This affects:
  - `SelectNextOccurrence` offsets and positions.
  - Any future “select all occurrences”.

This belongs both under “selection completeness” and “Unicode correctness”.

**b. Cursor/selection invariants can be violated by some editor moves**

- Invariants (editor.rs 472–488):

  ```rust
  debug_assert_eq!(cursor.to_position(), selection.head, "Cursor {} position must match selection head", i);
  ```

- But several non-shift movements update only the cursor, not the selection, e.g.:

  - `EditorMsg::SetCursorPosition` (update.rs 186–193):
    ```rust
    model.editor_mut().cursor_mut().line = line;
    model.editor_mut().cursor_mut().column = column;
    model.editor_mut().cursor_mut().desired_column = None;
    // selection is not touched here
    ```

- If there’s any existing selection, or if your `clear_selection` is not invoked by the caller beforehand, you now have `cursor.head != selection.head`, violating your own invariant.

- Fix (M):
  - For all **non-Shift** cursor moves (`MoveCursor`, Line/Doc start/end, `SetCursorPosition`, `PageUp/Down` without selection), either:
    - Call `editor.clear_selection()` after moving, or
    - Explicitly set `selection.anchor = selection.head = cursor.to_position()` for each cursor.
  - This also brings behavior in line with mainstream editors: arrow keys and clicks (without Shift) collapse selection.

**c. Multi-cursor vs selection semantics**

- You have `selections: Vec<Selection>` aligned to `cursors: Vec<Cursor>`, which is good.
- However, many handlers (examples in the snippet) still operate only on the primary selection (`selection()` / `selection_mut()`) rather than all selections. That’s fine for now but means:
  - A lot of commands are **not yet multi-cursor aware** (e.g., `SelectWord`, `SelectLine`, `SelectAll` likely only affect the primary cursor).
- For production, you probably want:
  - A clear policy: “X commands are per-primary-cursor only” vs “Y commands fan out to all cursors.”
  - Tests to confirm that e.g. typing, backspace, word-extend with selection all work across multiple cursors.

**d. Rectangle selection is incomplete**

- `StartRectangleSelection` / `UpdateRectangleSelection` (update.rs 449–480) correctly track `RectangleSelectionState`.
- `FinishRectangleSelection` begins to commit (482–501), but the core logic is truncated and clearly not done:

  ```rust
  let line_len = model.document().line_length(line);

  // Clamp columns to line length
  let start_col = top_left.c
  ```

- Issues to address (M):

  - Clamp both left and right columns per line based on `line_len` when building selections/cursors, especially for ragged-right rectangles.
  - Decide whether the rectangle selection should create:
    - Per-line **cursors only** (no selection, column at one side of rect).
    - Or true rectangular **selections** for each line.
  - Ensure commit logic respects multibyte characters (no byte-based indexing).

**e. “Production-ready” checklist for selection**

Once the above are fixed, the main remaining capabilities for a production editor would be:

- Selection granularity:
  - Word, subword, line, paragraph (you have some).
- Multiple independent ranges per cursor (you already support via multiple cursors + per-cursor selections).
- Consistent behavior between keyboard and mouse (click/drag vs Shift+click vs Shift+arrows).
- Selection preservation across undo/redo (see next section; currently only cursors are tracked in `EditOperation`).

Effort: M.

---

### 4. Undo/redo robustness (`EditOperation`)

**a. `EditOperation` is single-cursor and single-span**

- `Document::EditOperation` (document.rs 9–32) stores:

  - `position: usize` (single offset)
  - `text` or `deleted_text`/`inserted_text`
  - `cursor_before` / `cursor_after` (single `Cursor`)

- Consequences:

  1. **Multi-cursor edits become multiple independent undo steps.**
     - If typing with 3 cursors, and you push an `EditOperation` per cursor, `Undo` will only revert a single cursor’s change per key press.
     - This is usually not what users expect; they expect “one keypress = one undo”.
  2. Only the **primary cursor** is restored on undo/redo.
     - Secondary cursors/selections are not captured.
     - After undoing a multi-cursor edit, you likely end up with a single cursor in a state that doesn’t reflect the original multi-cursor context.

- Fix (L, but incremental path exists):

  - Introduce a notion of an **edit transaction**:
    - Either wrap `EditOperation` in a `Composite(Vec<EditOperation>)`,
    - Or maintain a separate `undo_groups: Vec<Range<usize>>` that group multiple `EditOperation`s into a single logical action.
  - Extend `EditOperation` to optionally store **multi-cursor snapshots** (e.g., a `Vec<Cursor>` before/after) for primary scenarios like “type over multiple selections”.

**b. Undo/redo and multi-view documents**

- `Document` holds `undo_stack`/`redo_stack`; `EditorState` is per-editor, and `EditorArea` allows the same `Document` to be used in multiple editors (see editor_area.rs 133–156 and tests).
- If `Undo` adjusts only the **focused editor’s** cursor based on `cursor_before`, other views of the same document will see the buffer change but their cursors won’t move. That’s acceptable but you should:

  - Document it as intentional (“undo is per-document but cursor restores only in the active view”), or
  - Consider also adjusting other `EditorState`s that point to this `DocumentId` (more complex).

**c. No coalescing of typing**

- `push_edit` (161–166) pushes every edit and clears redo:

  ```rust
  pub fn push_edit(&mut self, op: EditOperation) {
      self.undo_stack.push(op);
      self.redo_stack.clear();
      self.is_modified = true;
  }
  ```

- There’s no time-based or semantic coalescing (e.g., “typing a word” = one undo step).
- Fine for an MVP; for production, you’d eventually want:
  - Time-based grouping (e.g., within 500ms and same cursor region).
  - Or explicit grouping in your `update_document` logic.

Effort: M for grouping (without multi-cursor snapshots), L for full multi-cursor-aware undo.

---

### 5. Split view implementation (`EditorArea` / layout tree)

The split layout design is solid and idiomatic:

- `EditorArea` uses `LayoutNode::Group(GroupId) | Split(SplitContainer)` (editor_area.rs 123–127).
- `SplitContainer` uses `direction`, `children`, `ratios`, `min_sizes`.
- Tests `test_horizontal_split_layout`, `test_vertical_split_layout`, `test_group_at_point_split`, `test_splitter_at_point` (284–500) show correct propagation of rects and splitter locations.

A few points to tighten:

**a. Viewport vs group rect integration**

- `EditorGroup.rect` is set in `compute_layout` (not shown; tests use it).
- But `EditorState.viewport.visible_lines/columns` are updated only:
  - On app init (`EditorState::with_viewport` in `AppModel::new`, mod.rs 98–103),
  - On `AppModel::resize`/`set_char_width` for the **focused** editor (see section 1a).

For accurate scroll and cursor visibility across splits:

- After **layout changes** (splits, closing groups, window resize), you should:
  - Recompute `visible_lines/visible_columns` for each `EditorState` based on its group’s `Rect` and the global font metrics.
  - That function likely belongs on `EditorArea` (e.g., `fn sync_viewports(&mut self, line_height: usize, char_width: f32)`).

Effort: M.

**b. ID management is OK but keep constructors centralized**

- Tests construct new groups/editors/tabs by manually calling `next_group_id`, `next_editor_id`, `next_tab_id` (284–365).
- Ensure production code (LayoutMsg handlers) always goes through *one* API (e.g., `EditorArea::split_group`), which:
  - Creates the editor,
  - Assigns `EditorId/GroupId/TabId`,
  - Updates `layout` and `groups` and `editors`.
- That reduces the chance of inconsistent ID wiring.

Overall: split-view architecture is in good shape; remaining work is integration with viewport sizing and public APIs.

---

### 6. Other code smells, potential bugs, and architectural issues

**a. Unicode bugs in search/occurrence functions**

This is the most serious correctness problem beyond multi-cursor occurrence selection.

- `Document::find_all_occurrences` (document.rs 169–183):

  ```rust
  let haystack = self.buffer.to_string();
  // ...
  while let Some(pos) = haystack[start..].find(needle) {
      let abs_pos = start + pos;
      results.push((abs_pos, abs_pos + needle.len()));
      start = abs_pos + 1; // Allow overlapping matches
  }
  ```

- Issues:
  - `haystack[start..].find(needle)` returns **byte offsets** in the UTF‑8 `String`.
  - `needle.len()` is **bytes**, not chars.
  - Later, you treat these offsets as **character offsets** into the Rope:

    ```rust
    let (start_line, start_col) = self.offset_to_cursor(start_off);
    let (end_line, end_col) = self.offset_to_cursor(end_off);
    ```

    (`update.rs` 364–365 for `SelectNextOccurrence`)

- Consequences:
  - For non-ASCII text, positions are wrong: byte offsets ≠ char indices.
  - `char_to_line` in `offset_to_cursor` may get a “char index” that is actually in the middle of a codepoint or just too large, giving incorrect line/column or panicking in debug builds.

- Fix (M):

  - Keep everything in **char indices**:
    - Iterate over `haystack.chars()` and track a running `char_index`.
    - When you find a match, push `(char_index_start, char_index_end)`.
  - Or use `ropey`’s search facilities / implement a rope-based matcher directly.

**b. Unicode issues in `word_under_cursor`**

- `EditorState::word_under_cursor` (editor.rs 431–470):

  ```rust
  let line_content = document.get_line(cursor.line)?; // String
  // ...
  let col = cursor.column.min(line_content.len().saturating_sub(1));
  let chars: Vec<char> = line_content.chars().collect();
  if col >= chars.len() || char_type(chars[col]) != CharType::WordChar { ... }
  ```

- Problem:
  - `cursor.column` is a **char index** (as used in `cursor_to_offset`).
  - `line_content.len()` is **bytes**.
  - You clamp `col` to `len_bytes - 1`, then compare `col >= chars.len()`.
- Consequences:
  - For non-ASCII, `len_bytes > chars.len()`, so you may:
    - Clamp to a larger value than `chars.len() - 1`, causing `col >= chars.len()` and returning `None` even though you are on a word.
  - At least you won’t index out of bounds, but word detection and hence `SelectWord` and `SelectNextOccurrence` are broken for Unicode.

- Fix (S–M):

  ```rust
  let chars: Vec<char> = line_content.chars().collect();
  let col = cursor.column.min(chars.len().saturating_sub(1));
  ```

  - Avoid using `String::len()` for anything except byte slicing.

**c. `Document.buffer` is public**

- `Document` exposes `pub buffer: Rope` (document.rs 39).
- This makes it easy for other code to mutate the buffer without going through your undo/redo mechanisms.
- Fix (S):

  - Make `buffer` private and provide editing methods or keep all low-level mutations inside `document.rs` and `update.rs::update_document`.

**d. Minor performance considerations**

- `find_all_occurrences` clones the entire document (`self.buffer.to_string()`) on each search (169–176).
  - For now it’s acceptable; for large files, prefer:
    - Searching in chunks,
    - Or a Rope-based algorithm.
- `AppModel::new` uses placeholder metrics (`line_height = 20`, `char_width = 10.0`), then updates `char_width` later.
  - This is OK as long as you consistently recompute viewports after `set_char_width` (see 1a).

Effort: S–M for Unicode fixes; these should be prioritized.

---

3) Rationale and trade-offs

- I’ve focused on:
  - Clear, demonstrable bugs (Unicode index mismatches, `SelectNextOccurrence` logic, resize only updating focused editor).
  - Invariants that will bite you later (cursor/selection sync).
  - Architectural seams (undo grouping, viewport vs layout) that will be hard to retrofit if delayed too long.
- I did **not** recommend large structural changes because:
  - The Elm-style architecture is already good.
  - Most issues can be fixed incrementally within existing modules and patterns.
- Advanced undo/grouping and rope-native search are worthwhile, but can be layered on once correctness and basic UX match expectations.

---

4) Risks and guardrails

- **Unicode**: Until `find_all_occurrences` and `word_under_cursor` are fixed, non-ASCII text will misbehave. Treat the current state as “ASCII-only” and document that if needed.
- **Multi-cursor + undo**: Without grouping, power users will see surprising undo behavior. Guardrail: make “group by keystroke” a requirement before exposing multi-cursor widely.
- **Selection invariants**: If any debug-only assertions start firing as you add features, first check that every non-shift cursor movement is collapsing or re-syncing selections.
- **Multi-pane resizes**: As you wire up more layout behavior, add tests that change window size and verify all editors’ viewports update consistently.

---

5) When to consider the advanced path

Revisit architecture more deeply if:

- You add language-aware features (semantic selection, structural edit) that require rich undo transactions and logical operations beyond simple text diffing.
- Files grow into hundreds of MB, making the current `to_string` + `find` approach untenable.
- You expose scripting/plugins that need a stable selection/undo API.
- Multi-cursor becomes a primary selling point; then you’ll want fully multi-cursor-aware undo, selections, and commands.

---

6) Optional advanced path (brief)

If/when you decide to invest more:

- Introduce a **transactional edit log**:
  - `EditTransaction { ops: Vec<EditOperation>, cursors_before: Vec<Cursor>, cursors_after: Vec<Cursor> }`
  - `Document`’s undo/redo stacks store `EditTransaction`s, not single ops.
- Move search and text navigation onto **rope-native APIs**:
  - Avoid converting entire documents to `String`.
  - Implement word boundaries and searches directly on `Rope` with char indices.
- Encapsulate **selection and cursor state** behind a small API in `EditorState`:
  - No direct external mutation of `cursors`/`selections` without going through helper methods that enforce invariants.
- Extend layout with a “sync viewports from layout” pass whenever window or layout changes.

Scope-wise: the simple fixes (Unicode indexing, occurrence search offset, viewport-resize correctness, cursor/selection sync) are all S–M tasks and will materially improve robustness without changing the overall design.
```

**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01SvUkkALRWMDz717iR4ku7V`

```
Based on my comprehensive research of the three major production text editors, I can now provide you with a detailed analysis of their multi-cursor undo/redo batching strategies.

## Multi-Cursor Undo/Redo Batching: Production Patterns

Here's how **Zed**, **Helix**, and **VS Code** handle batching multi-cursor edits for atomic undo/redo:

### **1. Helix: Transaction-Based Composition (Simplest Pattern)**

[Helix's history system](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/master/helix-core/src/history.rs#L1-L100) uses a **single transaction per undo step**, regardless of multi-cursor count:

```rust
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct History {
    revisions: Vec<Revision>,
    current: usize,
}

struct Revision {
    parent: usize,
    last_child: Option<NonZeroUsize>,
    transaction: Transaction,      // ONE transaction per edit
    inversion: Transaction,         // Inverse for undo
    timestamp: Instant,
}
```

**Key insight**: Helix composes **all multi-cursor changes into a single `Transaction`** before committing to history. The [Transaction struct](https://github.com/helix-editor/helix/blob/master/helix-core/src/transaction.rs#L72-L78) contains a `ChangeSet` with all operations:

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ChangeSet {
    pub(crate) changes: Vec<Operation>,
    len: usize,
    len_after: usize,
}

pub type Change = (usize, usize, Option<Tendril>);
// All multi-cursor edits → single ChangeSet
```

**Batching approach**:
- Collect all cursor edits into a Vec of `Change` tuples
- Build one composite `ChangeSet` combining all ranges
- Store single `Transaction` in history with inverted version for undo
- Undo/redo applies entire transaction atomically

**Advantages**: Dead simple, minimal memory overhead

---

### **2. Zed: Lamport Timestamp Grouping + Operation Queue**

[Zed's approach](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/language/src/buffer.rs#L287-L332) separates **edit operations from selection updates** and uses **time-based batching**:

```rust
pub enum Operation {
    Buffer(text::Operation),  // Contains multiple edits per cursor

    UpdateSelections {
        selections: Arc<[Selection<Anchor>]>,
        lamport_timestamp: clock::Lamport,
        line_mode: bool,
        cursor_shape: CursorShape,
    },

    UpdateDiagnostics { ... },
    UpdateLineEnding { ... },
}
```

**Key batching mechanism** ([operation_queue.rs](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/text/src/operation_queue.rs#L1-L70)):

```rust
pub struct OperationQueue<T: Operation>(SumTree<OperationItem<T>>);

impl<T: Operation> OperationQueue<T> {
    pub fn insert(&mut self, mut ops: Vec<T>) {
        ops.sort_by_key(|op| op.lamport_timestamp());
        ops.dedup_by_key(|op| op.lamport_timestamp());
        self.0.edit(
            ops.into_iter()
                .map(|op| Edit::Insert(OperationItem(op)))
                .collect(),
            (),
        );
    }
}
```

**Batching strategy**:
1. **Time window batching**: Operations created within a `group_interval` are grouped
2. **Per-replica tracking**: Each replica has a Lamport timestamp; Zed deduplicates by timestamp
3. **Edit collections**: All multi-cursor edits in one action are collected into a single `EditOperation`

[In Editor::edit](https://github.com/zed-industries/zed/blob/main/crates/editor/src/editor.rs#L3608-L3620):
```rust
pub fn edit<I, S, T>(&mut self, edits: I, cx: &mut Context<Self>)
where
    I: IntoIterator<Item = (Range<S>, T)>,
    S: ToOffset,
    T: Into<Arc<str>>,
{
    self.buffer
        .update(cx, |buffer, cx| buffer.edit(edits, None, cx));
}
```

The `edits` iterator collects **all multi-cursor ranges** before creating a single `Buffer` operation.

**Time-based grouping example**:
```rust
// Vim test from codebase shows this:
cx.update_buffer(|buffer, _cx| {
    buffer.set_group_interval(Duration::from_millis(300))
});
```

**Advantages**:
- Collaborative editing friendly (Lamport timestamps)
- Automatic batching without explicit API calls
- Scales to many operations

---

### **3. VS Code: Edit Stack with Undo Groups**

VS Code (TypeScript) uses **explicit undo grouping** through a command pattern. While exact implementation varies, the pattern visible in the codebase is:

```typescript
// Conceptual pattern from VS Code architecture
executeEdits(
    source: string | null | undefined,
    edits: IIdentifiedSingleEditOperation[],
    endCursorState?: ICursorStateComputer | Selection[]
): boolean {
    // Multiple edits collected into single operation
    // Each edit identified by cursor
}
```

**Batching via**:
- `IIdentifiedSingleEditOperation[]` - array of identified operations
- Implicit grouping when executed in single `executeEdits` call
- Explicit groups via command grouping for LSP features

---

## **Recommended Rust Implementation Pattern**

Based on these patterns, here's a **hybrid approach optimized for Rust**:

```rust
/// Batch container for multi-cursor edits
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct EditBatch {
    /// All edits with their cursor indices
    edits: Vec<(Range<Offset>, String, usize)>, // (range, text, cursor_id)
    /// When this batch was created
    timestamp: Instant,
    /// Unique ID for undo/redo grouping
    batch_id: u64,
}

pub struct EditOperation {
    batch: EditBatch,
    /// For undo: the inverse changes
    inverse: EditBatch,
    /// Lamport timestamp for collaborative editing
    timestamp: Lamport,
}

pub struct UndoRedoManager {
    history: Vec<EditOperation>,
    current: usize,
    /// Time window for auto-batching
    group_interval: Duration,
    /// Last operation timestamp
    last_op_time: Instant,
}

impl UndoRedoManager {
    pub fn apply_multi_cursor_edits(
        &mut self,
        mut edits: Vec<(Range<Offset>, String, usize)>,
    ) -> Result<()> {
        // Sort edits by range to handle overlaps
        edits.sort_by_key(|(range, _, _)| range.start);

        // Check if should batch with previous operation
        let should_batch = Instant::now()
            .duration_since(self.last_op_time)
            < self.group_interval;

        if should_batch && !self.history.is_empty() {
            // Append to previous batch
            let last_op = self.history.last_mut().unwrap();
            last_op.batch.edits.extend(edits);
        } else {
            // Create new batch
            let batch = EditBatch {
                edits,
                timestamp: Instant::now(),
                batch_id: self.generate_id(),
            };

            let inverse = self.compute_inverse(&batch)?;
            self.history.push(EditOperation {
                batch,
                inverse,
                timestamp: Lamport::now(),
            });
            self.current = self.history.len() - 1;
        }

        self.last_op_time = Instant::now();
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn undo(&mut self) -> Option<EditBatch> {
        if self.current == 0 {
            return None;
        }

        self.current -= 1;
        Some(self.history[self.current].inverse.clone())
    }

    pub fn redo(&mut self) -> Option<EditBatch> {
        if self.current >= self.history.len() - 1 {
            return None;
        }

        self.current += 1;
        Some(self.history[self.current].batch.clone())
    }

    fn compute_inverse(&self, batch: &EditBatch) -> Result<EditBatch> {
        // Transform ranges in reverse order to compute inverse
        // This ensures undo works correctly with positional shifts
        Ok(EditBatch {
            edits: batch.edits.iter().rev()
                .map(|(range, text, cursor_id)| {
                    // Store deleted text and range
                    (range.clone(), String::new(), *cursor_id)
                })
                .collect(),
            timestamp: Instant::now(),
            batch_id: batch.batch_id,
        })
    }
}
```

---

## **Key Takeaways**

| Aspect | Helix | Zed | VS Code |
|--------|-------|-----|---------|
| **Batching Unit** | Single Transaction | EditOperation per time window | EditStack with groups |
| **Multi-cursor handling** | Composite ChangeSet | Separate edits, unified timestamp | IIdentifiedSingleEditOperation[] |
| **Undo atomicity** | Single inversion | Per-operation with timestamp | Group-based |
| **Scaling** | Scales to many edits in ChangeSet | Scales via Lamport ordering | Scales via command groups |
| **Collaborative** | Not built-in | Native (Lamport timestamps) | Via LSP |

**Best practice for your implementation**:
1. **Collect all multi-cursor edits** before creating any history entry
2. **Generate one batch ID** per user action (not per cursor)
3. **Store inverse operation** computed from original state
4. **Use time-window batching** (like Zed) if supporting collaborative editing
5. **Validate edit ranges** don't overlap before applying (handle reverse order)
```

1) TL;DR
Implement a series of tightly scoped fixes, starting with all Unicode/offset bugs and cursor–selection invariant issues, then making viewport handling multi-pane-aware, then adding a batch undo/redo op for multi-cursor edits, and finally completing occurrence selection and rectangle selection. Reuse existing structures (Document, EditorState, EditorArea, OccurrenceState, RectangleSelectionState) and add only one new enum variant plus small helper methods.

Below is a concrete, file-by-file plan.

---

## Priority 0 – Critical Bugs (S–M each, overall M–L)

### 0.1 Unicode byte/char mismatch in `Document::find_all_occurrences`

**Files / functions**

- `model/document.rs`
  - `fn find_all_occurrences(&self, needle: &str) -> Vec<(usize, usize)>`
  - `fn find_next_occurrence(&self, needle: &str, after_offset: usize) -> Option<(usize, usize)>`

**Goal**

Ensure `find_all_occurrences` and `find_next_occurrence` work in *character* offsets, consistent with the rest of the code (`cursor_to_offset`, `offset_to_cursor`, ropey APIs).

**Implementation steps**

1. **Clarify invariant**
   Decide and document: “Offsets in `Document` APIs are in *char indices*, not byte indices.” This matches `cursor_to_offset` / `offset_to_cursor`.

2. **Reimplement `find_all_occurrences` in char space**

   Current code:

   ```rust
   let haystack = self.buffer.to_string();
   ...
   while let Some(pos) = haystack[start..].find(needle) {
       let abs_pos = start + pos;
       results.push((abs_pos, abs_pos + needle.len())); // byte-based
   }
   ```

   Replace with something like (conceptually):

   - Early-return on empty `needle`.
   - Build a mapping from byte offset to char index:

     ```rust
     let haystack = self.buffer.to_string();
     let mut byte_to_char = std::collections::HashMap::new();
     let mut char_idx = 0;
     for (byte_idx, ch) in haystack.char_indices() {
         byte_to_char.insert(byte_idx, char_idx);
         char_idx += 1;
     }
     let total_chars = char_idx;
     let needle_char_len = needle.chars().count();
     ```

   - Then run the substring search in bytes as before, but convert each match:

     ```rust
     let mut results = Vec::new();
     let mut start_byte = 0;
     while let Some(rel) = haystack[start_byte..].find(needle) {
         let match_start_byte = start_byte + rel;
         let match_end_byte = match_start_byte + needle.len();
         let start_char = *byte_to_char.get(&match_start_byte).unwrap();
         let end_char = start_char + needle_char_len;
         results.push((start_char, end_char));
         start_byte = match_start_byte + 1; // allow overlaps
     }
     ```

   - If you don’t want a HashMap, you can instead walk `char_indices` once, keeping parallel vectors of `(byte, char_idx)` and doing a binary search for `match_start_byte`; but HashMap is simplest for now.

3. **Update `find_next_occurrence` to treat `after_offset` as char offset**

   Currently it:

   ```rust
   let occurrences = self.find_all_occurrences(needle);
   if let Some(&occ) = occurrences.iter().find(|(start, _)| *start > after_offset) { ... }
   ```

   This is fine once `find_all_occurrences` is fixed; just ensure we document that `after_offset` is char-offset.

4. **Tests to add (unit tests in `document.rs`)**

   - A test with pure ASCII:
     - buffer `"abc abc abc"`, needle `"abc"`, assert char offsets `(0,3), (4,7), (8,11)`.
   - A test with multi-byte characters:
     - buffer `"äbc äbc"` (where `ä` is multi-byte), needle `"äbc"`, assert that:
       - First occurrence maps to `(0, 3)` in char indices.
       - Second occurrence maps to `(4, 7)` in char indices.
   - A test where the needle itself contains multi-byte chars and occurs multiple times.
   - A test verifying `find_next_occurrence` wraps correctly and uses char offsets.

**Dependencies**

- Needed by:
  - `SelectNextOccurrence`
  - `SelectAllOccurrences` (Priority 3)
- Fix this before implementing any “occurrence” selection features.

### 0.2 Unicode bug in `EditorState::word_under_cursor`

**Files / functions**

- `model/editor.rs`
  - `fn word_under_cursor(&self, document: &Document) -> Option<(String, Position, Position)>`

**Issue**

- Uses `line_content.len()` (bytes) to clamp column, then indexes into `Vec<char>` by that byte-based index.

**Implementation steps**

1. **Operate fully in char indices**

   Current:

   ```rust
   let line_content = document.get_line(cursor.line)?;
   let line_content = line_content.trim_end_matches('\n');
   let col = cursor.column.min(line_content.len().saturating_sub(1));
   let chars: Vec<char> = line_content.chars().collect();
   if col >= chars.len() || char_type(chars[col]) != CharType::WordChar {
       return None;
   }
   ...
   ```

   Replace with:

   ```rust
   let line = document.get_line(cursor.line)?;
   let line = line.trim_end_matches('\n');
   if line.is_empty() {
       return None;
   }
   let chars: Vec<char> = line.chars().collect();
   if chars.is_empty() {
       return None;
   }

   let col = cursor.column.min(chars.len().saturating_sub(1));
   if char_type(chars[col]) != CharType::WordChar {
       return None;
   }
   ```

2. **Keep boundary loops unchanged (they already operate in char indices)**

   The while loops over `start` and `end` are fine once `col` is char-based.

3. **Tests to add**

   - Cursor on ASCII word.
   - Cursor on a word containing `ä`, `é`, or emoji.
   - Cursor at end-of-line when last character is multi-byte.
   - Cursor on whitespace / punctuation around Unicode words to ensure None.

**Dependencies**

- `SelectNextOccurrence` uses `word_under_cursor`; fix first.

---

### 0.3 `SelectNextOccurrence` using wrong offset

**Files / functions**

- `update.rs`
  - `EditorMsg::SelectNextOccurrence` branch in `fn update_editor(...)`

**Issue**

- Uses cursor position as search origin instead of the end of the last occurrence / last selection, causing re-selection of the same occurrence and misbehavior, especially with multiple cursors.

**Implementation steps**

1. **Ensure we always search from the end of the current “occurrence chain”**

   Replace:

   ```rust
   let cursor = model.editor().cursor();
   let current_offset = model.document().cursor_to_offset(cursor.line, cursor.column);
   ```

   with logic:

   ```rust
   // Determine starting offset:
   let doc = model.document();
   let editor = model.editor();

   let start_offset = if let Some(state) = &editor.occurrence_state {
       // Continue from last search offset
       state.last_search_offset
   } else {
       // Initial call: use end of current primary selection
       let sel = editor.selection();
       let end = sel.end();
       doc.cursor_to_offset(end.line, end.column)
   };
   ```

   Then call:

   ```rust
   if let Some((start_off, end_off)) = doc.find_next_occurrence(&search_text, start_offset) {
       ...
       state.last_search_offset = end_off;
   }
   ```

2. **Use the fixed char-based offsets**

   Once `find_next_occurrence` returns char offsets, the subsequent `offset_to_cursor` calls are correct.

3. **Consider wrapping semantics**

   - If `find_next_occurrence` wraps to the first occurrence and that occurrence is already selected (we detect via `already_selected`), show “No more occurrences” and **do not modify** cursors.
   - This is already mostly implemented; just make sure the logic uses the new `start_offset` and `last_search_offset`.

4. **Tests to add (integration-level, in `update.rs` or a separate `tests` module)**

   - Start with a single selection; call `SelectNextOccurrence` repeatedly and assert that:
     - Each call adds a new cursor at the next occurrence.
     - After the last occurrence, you get the “No more occurrences” message and no new cursors.
   - Same scenarios with text containing Unicode; ensure we don’t get stuck on the same word.

**Dependencies**

- Relies on 0.1 and 0.2 being correct.

---

### 0.4 Cursor/selection invariant violations in non‑shift movements

**Files / functions**

- `update.rs`
  - `EditorMsg::MoveCursor`
  - `MoveCursorLineStart`, `MoveCursorLineEnd`
  - `MoveCursorDocumentStart`, `MoveCursorDocumentEnd`
  - `MoveCursorWord`
  - `PageUp`, `PageDown`
  - `SetCursorPosition`
- `model/editor.rs`
  - `EditorState` (add helper method)

**Issue**

- In non-shift movements we update cursors but *not* selections, breaking the invariant asserted in `EditorState::assert_invariants` that each selection’s head equals its cursor position.

**Implementation steps**

1. **Add a helper to `EditorState` to sync selections with cursors**

   In `EditorState` (editor.rs):

   ```rust
   impl EditorState {
       /// Collapse all selections so that anchor == head == cursor position for each cursor.
       pub fn collapse_selections_to_cursors(&mut self) {
           for (cursor, selection) in self.cursors.iter().zip(self.selections.iter_mut()) {
               let pos = cursor.to_position();
               selection.anchor = pos;
               selection.head = pos;
           }
       }
   }
   ```

   If you already have `clear_selection()` doing exactly this for all cursors, just use that instead; otherwise refactor `clear_selection()` to use this helper.

2. **Call this helper after *every* non-shift cursor move**

   In `update_editor`, for each of:

   - `MoveCursor(Direction::*)`
   - `MoveCursorLineStart`
   - `MoveCursorLineEnd`
   - `MoveCursorDocumentStart`
   - `MoveCursorDocumentEnd`
   - `MoveCursorWord`
   - `PageUp`
   - `PageDown`
   - `SetCursorPosition`

   After you finish mutating `cursor_mut()`, add:

   ```rust
   model.editor_mut().collapse_selections_to_cursors();
   ```

   before `ensure_cursor_visible` or at least before returning.

   This preserves multi-cursor *positions* but clears selection extents (matching typical editor behavior: arrow keys without Shift drop selection).

3. **Tests to add**

   - With a non-empty primary selection, press `MoveCursorLeft`:
     - Assert: primary selection is empty and aligned with cursor.
   - With multiple cursors each having selections, press `MoveCursorRight`:
     - Assert: all selections collapsed to their respective cursors, no panics in debug.
   - Add a debug-only test that calls `assert_invariants()` after a battery of non-shift moves.

**Dependencies**

- None, but improves robustness before layering more features.

### 1.2 Sync viewports after layout changes

**Files / functions**

- `model/editor_area.rs`
  - `fn compute_layout(&mut self, available: Rect) -> Vec<SplitterBar>` (or equivalent)
- `model/mod.rs` (`AppModel`)
  - Possibly add a helper to recompute visible_lines/columns per group.

**Issue**

- When creating or adjusting splits, group `rect`s change, but editor viewports are not recomputed from the new sizes.

**Implementation steps**

1. **Add a helper on `EditorArea` to apply viewport dimensions for a group**

   New method in `EditorArea`:

   ```rust
   impl EditorArea {
       pub fn update_group_viewports(
           &mut self,
           group_id: GroupId,
           line_height: usize,
           char_width: f32,
       ) {
           let group_rect = match self.groups.get(&group_id) {
               Some(g) => g.rect,
               None => return,
           };

           // Compute visible_lines and visible_columns from group_rect
           let visible_lines = (group_rect.height as usize) / line_height;
           // Subtract gutter and padding similar to AppModel::new
           let text_x = crate::model::text_start_x(char_width).round();
           let available_width = group_rect.width - text_x;
           let visible_columns = if available_width > 0.0 {
               (available_width / char_width).floor() as usize
           } else {
               0
           };

           // Apply to all editors in this group
           let editor_ids: Vec<_> = self.groups[&group_id]
               .tabs
               .iter()
               .map(|t| t.editor_id)
               .collect();
           for editor_id in editor_ids {
               if let Some(editor) = self.editors.get_mut(&editor_id) {
                   editor.resize_viewport(visible_lines, visible_columns);
               }
           }
       }
   }
   ```

   (You might choose to move `text_start_x` usage into AppModel; this is a plan, not final code.)

2. **Call this helper after layout recomputation**

   In `EditorArea::compute_layout` (or right after it in whoever calls it, likely in `update_layout`), when you know all groups’ `rect`s are updated, walk all groups:

   ```rust
   for group_id in self.groups.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>() {
       self.update_group_viewports(group_id, line_height, char_width);
   }
   ```

   You’ll need to pass `line_height` and `char_width` down from `AppModel` through `update_layout` into `compute_layout` or into a post-layout sync step.

3. **Tests to add**

   - Create a horizontal split, call `compute_layout`, and assert:
     - Upper group has `visible_lines` consistent with its `rect.height`.
     - Both groups have `visible_columns` consistent with `rect.width`.
   - Resize the window / recompute layout and assert viewports update.

**Dependencies**

- Uses `text_start_x`, `line_height`, and `char_width` from `AppModel`.

### 1.3 Avoid document cloning in `AppModel::ensure_cursor_visible*`

**Files / functions**

- `model/mod.rs` (`AppModel`)
  - `fn ensure_cursor_visible(&mut self)`
  - `fn ensure_cursor_visible_directional(&mut self, vertical_up: Option<bool>)`

**Issue**

- Both methods currently clone the focused `Document` just to pass an immutable reference to `EditorState::ensure_cursor_visible*`, causing unnecessary overhead.

**Implementation steps**

1. **Add a method on `EditorArea` to ensure focused cursor visibility without cloning**

   In `EditorArea`:

   ```rust
   impl EditorArea {
       pub fn ensure_focused_cursor_visible(&mut self, mode: ScrollRevealMode) {
           let doc_id = match self.focused_document_id() {
               Some(id) => id,
               None => return,
           };

           // Scope the borrows to avoid double mutable aliasing:
           let (doc_ptr, editor_id) = {
               let doc = self.documents.get(&doc_id).unwrap() as *const Document;
               let editor_id = self.focused_editor_id().unwrap();
               (doc, editor_id)
           };

           // Safe because we only use immutable doc reference with a mutable editor.
           let doc_ref = unsafe { &*doc_ptr };
           if let Some(editor) = self.editors.get_mut(&editor_id) {
               editor.ensure_cursor_visible_with_mode(doc_ref, mode);
           }
       }
   }
   ```

   (You may prefer a different, purely safe borrowing strategy using separate blocks; the key idea is: let EditorArea own both, avoid cloning.)

2. **Refactor `AppModel::ensure_cursor_visible*` to delegate**

   - `ensure_cursor_visible`:

     ```rust
     pub fn ensure_cursor_visible(&mut self) {
         self.editor_area.ensure_focused_cursor_visible(ScrollRevealMode::Minimal);
     }
     ```

   - `ensure_cursor_visible_directional`:

     ```rust
     pub fn ensure_cursor_visible_directional(&mut self, vertical_up: Option<bool>) {
         let mode = match vertical_up {
             Some(true) => ScrollRevealMode::TopAligned,
             Some(false) => ScrollRevealMode::BottomAligned,
             None => ScrollRevealMode::Minimal,
         };
         self.editor_area.ensure_focused_cursor_visible(mode);
     }
     ```

3. **Remove the `.clone()` calls on `Document`.**

4. **Tests**

   - No specific behavior change; you mainly want to ensure all existing tests still pass and maybe add a perf-related smoke test on a large document to ensure no clone.

**Dependencies**

- Small refactor; safe once viewports are otherwise correct.

## Priority 2 – Multi-Cursor Undo/Redo (M–L effort)

### 2.1 Add `EditOperation::Batch` variant

**Files / functions**

- `model/document.rs`
  - `enum EditOperation`
  - `impl Document` – undo/redo implementation (not shown in snippets but assumed present)

**Implementation steps**

1. **Extend `EditOperation`**

   Add:

   ```rust
   pub enum EditOperation {
       Insert { ... },
       Delete { ... },
       Replace { ... },
       Batch {
           operations: Vec<EditOperation>,
           cursors_before: Vec<Cursor>,
           cursors_after: Vec<Cursor>,
       },
   }
   ```

   - Keep `operations` ordered in the *forward* application order.
   - The nested operations are *per-cursor* inserts/deletes (no nested Batch to keep it simple).

2. **Update undo logic**

   In `Document::undo` (wherever implemented):

   - When popping an `EditOperation::Batch`:
     - Undo nested `operations` in *reverse* order, just like a transaction:
       - For each nested op, reuse existing undo logic (Insert ↔ Delete, Replace reversed).
     - Restore `cursors_before` on the associated `EditorState`. To do this, you’ll need the undo entry to be applied in a context where you can touch both `Document` and `EditorState`. If undo is currently purely in `Document`, extend its API so the caller (update.rs) passes in a mutable `EditorState` (or a closure) to restore cursor snapshots.

   Simpler pattern:

   - Modify the undo API signature:

     ```rust
     pub fn undo(&mut self, editor: &mut EditorState)
     ```

   - For non-Batch operations, set `editor.cursors` based on `cursor_before` / `cursor_after` as currently done.
   - For `Batch`, set:

     ```rust
     editor.cursors = cursors_before.clone();
     // Also ensure selections are aligned with cursors
     editor.collapse_selections_to_cursors();
     ```

3. **Update redo logic**

   - Mirror undo: for `Batch`, reapply `operations` in forward order and restore `cursors_after`.

4. **Consider selections**

   - Minimal version: only snapshot cursors; recompute selections by collapsing to cursors on undo/redo.
   - If you want full fidelity, you could extend snapshots to store `Vec<Selection>` as well; but that’s more complex and likely YAGNI for now. Start minimal.

5. **Tests**

   - Single-cursor operations must still round-trip (Insert/Delete/Replace unaffected).
   - A multi-cursor insert:
     - Start with 3 cursors on different lines.
     - Insert a character (so 3 inserts).
     - Undo once: all three insertions gone, cursors back where they were.
     - Redo: all three re-applied.

---

### 2.2 Use `Batch` for multi-cursor edits

**Files / functions**

- `update.rs`
  - `update_document` handling:
    - `DocumentMsg::InsertChar`
    - `InsertNewline`
    - `DeleteBackward`
    - `DeleteForward`
    - `DeleteLine`
    - `Duplicate`
    - Any other multi-cursor-aware edits.

**Implementation steps**

1. **Detect when we have multiple cursors**

   In each editing path, start with something like:

   ```rust
   let multi = model.editor().cursors.len() > 1;
   ```

2. **Collect per-cursor operations**

   Instead of directly editing `document`/`buffer` in a loop and pushing one op per cursor, do:

   - Take snapshots:

     ```rust
     let cursors_before = model.editor().cursors.clone();
     ```

   - For each cursor, perform the edit and collect an individual `EditOperation` (Insert/Delete/Replace) that describes what happened for that cursor.

   - After all per-cursor edits:

     ```rust
     let cursors_after = model.editor().cursors.clone();
     if multi {
         document.push_edit(EditOperation::Batch {
             operations,
             cursors_before,
             cursors_after,
         });
     } else {
         document.push_edit(operations.into_iter().next().unwrap());
     }
     ```

3. **Preserve existing single-cursor semantics**

   - Ensure that for `multi == false`, behavior and undo/redo entries are identical to current implementation.

4. **Tests**

   - Same as in 2.1, but now end-to-end through `update_document` and `DocumentMsg::Undo`/`Redo`.

**Dependencies**

- Requires 2.1 (`Batch` variant and undo/redo handling) to be in place.

#### 3.2.2 Implement `SelectAllOccurrences`

       new_cursors.push(Cursor::at(end_line, end_col));
       new_selections.push(Selection::from_positions(start_pos, end_pos));

4. **Initialize `OccurrenceState`**

     editor.occurrence_state = Some(OccurrenceState {
         search_text: search_text.clone(),

## Priority 4 – Rectangle Selection (M effort)

### 4.1 Complete `FinishRectangleSelection` logic

**Files / functions**

- `update.rs`
  - `EditorMsg::FinishRectangleSelection` branch
- `model/editor.rs`
  - `RectangleSelectionState`

**Desired semantics**

- While dragging, `preview_cursors` show vertical column positions across lines.
- On finish:
  - We create one cursor per line from `top_left.line` to `bottom_right.line`.
  - If `start.column != current.column`, selections are created as horizontal spans between those columns on each line (clamped to line length).
  - Primary cursor should end at the drag end position.

**Implementation steps**

1. **Finish the loop body in `FinishRectangleSelection`**

   Current skeleton:

   ```rust
   let top_left = model.editor().rectangle_selection.top_left();
   let bottom_right = model.editor().rectangle_selection.bottom_right();
   let cursor_col = model.editor().rectangle_selection.current.column;

   model.editor_mut().cursors.clear();
   model.editor_mut().selections.clear();

   for line in top_left.line..=bottom_right.line {
       let line_len = model.document().line_length(line);
       // TODO: compute start/end cols and push cursors/selections
   }
   ```

2. **Compute start/end columns per line**

   Let:

   ```rust
   let start_col = top_left.column;
   let end_col = bottom_right.column;
   ```

   For each line:

   ```rust
   let line_len = model.document().line_length(line);
   let clamped_start = start_col.min(line_len);
   let clamped_end = end_col.min(line_len);

   let pos_start = Position::new(line, clamped_start);
   let pos_end = Position::new(line, clamped_end);
   let cursor_pos = Position::new(line, cursor_col.min(line_len));
   ```

   - If `start_col == end_col`:
     - We just want multiple cursors, no selection:

       ```rust
       let cursor = Cursor::from_position(cursor_pos);
       let selection = Selection::new(cursor_pos);
       ```

   - Otherwise:
     - For each line, create a selection from `pos_start` to `pos_end`.
     - Decide anchor/head based on drag direction:

       ```rust
       let (anchor, head) = if model.editor().rectangle_selection.start.column <=
                                 model.editor().rectangle_selection.current.column
                           {
                               (pos_start, pos_end)
                           } else {
                               (pos_end, pos_start)
                           };
       let mut selection = Selection::from_anchor_head(anchor, head);
       // Head must match cursor position:
       selection.head = cursor_pos;
       ```

     - Cursor at `cursor_pos`.

3. **Push all cursors/selections**

   ```rust
   let editor = model.editor_mut();
   for line in top_left.line..=bottom_right.line {
       // compute as above...
       editor.cursors.push(Cursor::from_position(cursor_pos));
       editor.selections.push(selection);
   }
   editor.deduplicate_cursors();
   editor.rectangle_selection.active = false;
   editor.rectangle_selection.preview_cursors.clear();
   ```

4. **Ensure cursor visible**

   - After the loop, ensure the last cursor is visible:

     ```rust
     model.ensure_cursor_visible();
     ```

5. **Handle `CancelRectangleSelection`**

   - In `EditorMsg::CancelRectangleSelection`, simply:

     ```rust
     model.editor_mut().rectangle_selection.active = false;
     model.editor_mut().rectangle_selection.preview_cursors.clear();
     ```

   - Do *not* alter existing cursors/selections.

6. **Tests**

   - Drag from (line 2, col 4) to (line 5, col 10) on text with varying line lengths:
     - Each resulting selection clamped to that line’s length.
   - Drag with equal start and end columns:
     - Multiple cursors only, no selections.
   - Drag from right to left (start.col > current.col):
     - Selections reversed but cursor at current position.

---

## Dependencies & Ordering

1. **Priority 0 (critical)**
   - Fix Unicode/offset bugs (`Document::find_all_occurrences`, `word_under_cursor`).
   - Fix `SelectNextOccurrence` search origin.
   - Fix cursor/selection invariants after non-shift moves.
   - These unblock all selection and search behavior.

2. **Priority 1 (multi-pane)**
   - Make resize and layout multi-pane-aware and remove document clone.
   - This stabilizes visual behavior before adding more complex undo/redo.

3. **Priority 2 (multi-cursor undo/redo)**
   - Introduce `EditOperation::Batch`.
   - Rewire multi-cursor editing paths to use it.

4. **Priority 3 (selection phase 9)**
   - Finalize selection APIs and occurrence commands (`SelectNextOccurrence`, `SelectAllOccurrences`, `UnselectOccurrence`).

5. **Priority 4 (rectangle selection)**
   - Implement final rectangle selection commit semantics.

---

## Rationale & Trade-offs

- **Simplicity-first**: Use minimal new types (only `Batch` variant) and keep data localized to existing structs (`EditorState`, `OccurrenceState`, `RectangleSelectionState`).
- **Char-based invariants**: Unifying everything on char offsets avoids a long tail of Unicode bugs.
- **Minimal borrowing changes**: Instead of redesigning ownership, use an `EditorArea` helper to avoid cloning `Document` while still satisfying the borrow checker.
- **Undo/redo**: Wrapping multi-cursor edits into `Batch` keeps history semantics intuitive without rethinking the full undo stack.

---

## Risks & Guardrails

- **Performance**: `find_all_occurrences` may scan the full buffer and build byte→char maps; acceptable for now, but revisit if you hit large-file performance issues.
- **Unsafe code (if used for borrows)**: If you use `unsafe` for the doc/editor borrow, encapsulate it in a very small, well-documented helper and test thoroughly.
- **Invariant complexity**: Ensure `collapse_selections_to_cursors()` is consistently used after non-shift moves; forgetting it in one path will reintroduce bugs.

---

## When to Consider the Advanced Path

- If documents routinely exceed tens of MB, consider:
  - Rope-native search instead of materializing `String`.
  - Streaming search with a proper char offset iterator.
- If multi-cursor editing becomes more complex (e.g., structural edits), consider:
  - A richer undo transaction model instead of just `Batch`.
- If you add more layout features (e.g., variable line heights), reconsider the viewport sync to be more layout-aware.

---

## Optional Advanced Path (Outline Only)

- Replace the string-based search with a rope-based KMP or similar algorithm, iterating over `Rope::chars()` and returning char offsets directly.
- Extend `Batch` to hold both cursor and selection snapshots so undo/redo restores exact selection shapes.
- Model multi-pane layout with per-group viewport structs and a dedicated layout-to-viewport sync phase that can handle gutter widths, rulers, and minimap space separately.

This plan should be implementable incrementally, with each priority level shippable on its own and covered by targeted tests.
```

`model/document.rs`\n\n```rust\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub enum EditOperation {\n    Insert {\n        position: usize,\n        text: String,\n        cursor_before: Cursor,\n        cursor_after: Cursor,\n    },\n    Delete {\n        position: usize,\n        text: String,\n        cursor_before: Cursor,\n        cursor_after: Cursor,\n    },\n    Replace {\n        position: usize,\n        deleted_text: String,\n        inserted_text: String,\n        cursor_before: Cursor,\n        cursor_after: Cursor,\n    },\n    // NEW\n    Batch {\n        operations: Vec<EditOperation>,\n        cursors_before: Vec<Cursor>,\n        cursors_after: Vec<Cursor>,\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### 2.2 Update Undo/Redo Handling\n\n```rust\nimpl Document {\n    pub fn undo(&mut self, editor: &mut EditorState) -> bool {\n        let op = match self.undo_stack.pop() {\n            Some(op) => op,\n            None => return false,\n        };\n        \n        match &op {\n            EditOperation::Batch { operations, cursors_before, .. } => {\n                // Undo in reverse order\n                for inner_op in operations.iter().rev() {\n                    self.apply_undo_single(inner_op);\n                }\n                // Restore cursor state\n                editor.cursors = cursors_before.clone();\n                editor.collapse_selections_to_cursors();\n            }\n            _ => {\n                self.apply_undo_single(&op);\n                // Restore single cursor\n                if let Some(cursor) = op.cursor_before() {\n                    editor.cursors[0] = cursor;\n                    editor.collapse_selections_to_cursors();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        \n        self.redo_stack.push(op);\n        self.is_modified = true;\n        true\n    }\n    \n    fn apply_undo_single(&mut self, op: &EditOperation) {\n        match op {\n            EditOperation::Insert { position, text, .. } => {\n                let end = position + text.chars().count();\n                self.buffer.remove(*position..end);\n            }\n            EditOperation::Delete { position, text, .. } => {\n                self.buffer.insert(*position, text);\n            }\n            EditOperation::Replace { position, deleted_text, inserted_text, .. } => {\n                let end = position + inserted_text.chars().count();\n                self.buffer.remove(*position..end);\n                self.buffer.insert(*position, deleted_text);\n            }\n            EditOperation::Batch { .. } => {\n                // Nested batches not supported\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 2.3 Use Batch in Multi-Cursor Edits\n\n**File:** `update.rs` - InsertChar, DeleteBackward, etc.\n\n```rust\n// Example for InsertChar with position */,\n        });\n        \n        // Update cursor\n        // ...\n    }\n    \n    let cursors_after = model.editor().cursors.clone();\n    \n    // Push as batch or single\n    if multi {\n        model.document_mut().push_edit(EditOperation::Batch {\n            operations,\n            cursors_before,\n            cursors_after,\n        });\n    } else {\n        model.document_mut().push_edit(operations.into_iter().next().unwrap());\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## Priority 3: Selection Phase 9\n\n**Effort:** M (Medium)\n**Files:** `model/editor.rs`, `update.rs`, Some(Cmd::Redraw),\n            }\n        }\n    };\n    \n    if search_text.is_empty() {\n        return Some(Cmd::Redraw);\n    }\n    \n    // Find all occurrences\n    let occurrences = model.document().find_all_occurrences(&search_text);\n    \n    if occurrences.is_empty() {\n        return Some(Cmd::Redraw);\n    }\n    \n    // Build cursors and selections\n    let mut new_cursors = Vec::new();\n    let mut new_selections = Vec::new();\n    \n    for (start_off, end_off) in &occurrences {\n        let (start_line, start_col) = start_col);\n        let end_pos = Position::new(end_line, end_col);\n        \n        new_cursors.push(Cursor::at(end_line, end_col));\n        new_selections.push(Selection::from_anchor_head(start_pos, end_pos));\n    }\n    \n    // Replace editor state\n    let editor = model.editor_mut();\n    editor.cursors = new_cursors;\n    editor.selections = new_selections;\n    editor.deduplicate_cursors();\n    \n    // Set up occurrence state\n    editor.occurrence_state = Some(OccurrenceState {\n        search_text,\n        added_cursor_indices: (0..editor.cursors.len()).collect(),\n        last_search_offset: occurrences.last().map(|(_, e)| *e).unwrap_or(0),\n    });\n    \n    model.ensure_cursor_visible();\n    Some(Cmd::Redraw)\n}\n```\n\n### 3.3 Add UnselectOccurrence Message\n\n**File:** `messages.rs` - Already exists, verify handler in `update.rs`\n\nThe handler should:\n1. FinishRectangleSelection\n\n**File:** `update.rs`\n\n```rust\nEditorMsg::FinishRectangleSelection => {\n    if !model.editor().rectangle_selection.active {\n        return Some(Cmd::Redraw);\n    }\n    \n    let rect_state = &model.editor().rectangle_selection;\n    let top_left = rect_state.top_left();\n    let bottom_right = rect_state.bottom_right();\n    let start_col = rect_state.start.column;\n    let end_col = rect_state.current.column;\n    \n    // Determine if we have a selection or just cursors\n    let has_selection = start_col != end_col;\n    let (left_col, right_col) = if start_col <= end_col {\n        (start_col, end_col)\n    } else {\n        (end_col, start_col)\n    };\n    \n    let mut new_cursors = Vec::new();\n    let mut new_selections = Vec::new();\n    \n    for line in top_left.line..=bottom_right.line {\n        let line_len = model.document().line_length(line);\n        \n        // Clamp columns to line length\n        let clamped_left = left_col.min(line_len);\n        let clamped_right = right_col.min(line_len);\n        let cursor_col = end_col.min(line_len);\n        \n        let cursor = Cursor::at(line, cursor_col);\n        \n        let selection = if has_selection && clamped_left < clamped_right {\n            let anchor = Position::new(line, clamped_left);\n            let head = Position::new(line, clamped_right);\n            \n            // Respect drag direction\n            if start_col <= end_col {\n                Selection::from_anchor_head(anchor, head)\n            } else {\n                Selection::from_anchor_head(head, anchor)\n            }\n        } else {\n            Selection::new(Position::new(line, cursor_col))\n        };\n        \n        new_cursors.push(cursor);\n        new_selections.push(selection);\n    }\n    \n    // Apply to editor\n    let editor = model.editor_mut();\n    editor.cursors = new_cursors;\n    editor.selections = new_selections;\n    editor.deduplicate_cursors();\n    \n    // Clear rectangle state\n    editor.rectangle_selection.active = false;\n    editor.rectangle_selection.preview_cursors.clear();\n Unicode-safe search\n- `find_next_occurrence_*` (2 tests) - Wrap-around behavior\n- `test_word_under_cursor_*` (6 tests) - Unicode word detection\n- `test_select_next_occurrence_*` (2 tests) - Multi-cursor occurrence selection\n- `test_select_all_occurrences_*` (2 tests) - Select all matching words\n- `test_*_clears_selection` (3 tests) - Cursor/selection invariant tests\n\n---\n\n## Code Changes Summary\n\n### Files Modified:\n1. **`src/model/document.rs`**\n   - `find_all_occurrences()` - Unicode-safe byte→char mapping\n   - `EditOperation::Batch` - New variant for multi-cursor undo\n\n2. **`src/model/editor.rs`**\n   - `word_under_cursor()` - Fixed Unicode column handling\n   - `collapse_selections_to_cursors()` - New helper method\n\n3. **`src/model/editor_area.rs`**\n   - `sync_all_viewports()` - Sync viewports from group rects\n   - `ensure_focused_cursor_visible()` - Avoid document cloning\n\n4. **`src/model/mod.rs`**\n   - `resize()` - Updates ALL editors now\n   - `set_char_width()` - Updates ALL editors now\n   - `ensure_cursor_visible*()` - Delegates to EditorArea\n\n5. **`src/update.rs`**\n   - All non-shift movement handlers call `collapse_selections_to_cursors()`\n   - `SelectNextOccurrence` - Uses `last_search_offset`\n   - `SelectAllOccurrences` - Fully implemented\n   - `Undo/Redo` - Handles `EditOperation::Batch`\n   - `apply_undo/redo_operation*()` - New helper functions\n\n---\n\n## Remaining Work\n\n### Still TODO:\n1. **P2.3: Use Batch for multi-cursor edits** (Low priority)\n   - Time-based undo grouping\n\n---\n\n## Architecture Improvements Made\n\n1. **Unicode-safe everywhere** - All search/selection uses char indices\n2. **Multi-pane ready** - Viewport sync, all-editor updates\n3. **No document cloning** -
